Ralph Lauren: 3 Key Takeaways for Long-Term Investors

📅 Nov 15, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Pulling back from lower-tier doors trades short-term volume for brand equity, a tradeoff the article's third takeaway leaves unresolved.

On its second-quarter earnings call, held on Nov. 5, 2026, Ralph Lauren (RL -1.15%) announced that it raised full-year guidance after quarterly revenue and profit beat internal targets. The apparel maker is expanding margins by selling more goods at full price and building its luxury presence across global markets. That pricing power protects profitability even when store traffic slows in regions such as Europe. Three things management said stand out.

Asia has become the primary growth engine for Ralph Lauren, driven by direct-to-consumer (DTC) sales, which include company-owned stores and websites. Revenue in China rose by over 35% as management expanded its retail ecosystem in top cities.

Ralph Lauren increased its Average Unit Retail (AUR), the average price paid per item, by 9% during the quarter. That pricing power helped boost adjusted gross margin, the percentage of revenue kept after direct costs, to 70%.

To protect its premium positioning, Ralph Lauren is pulling back from off-price channels, which sell excess stock at deep discounts. Management is intentionally exiting lower-tier department stores to focus on higher-margin, full-price sales.

Marketing investments reached 8.6% of sales as Ralph Lauren funded major campaign activations. Brand storytelling drives customer acquisition across channels, and management intends to maintain high reinvestment levels alongside margin growth.

Pricing discipline, retail channel expansion in Asia, and direct customer engagement anchor future value as management navigates traffic headwinds in Europe.

What A Road Sign With A Black 'Y' Means (And Other Letters, Too)

📅 Nov 15, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

We have covered all manner of weird road signs on Jalopnik before, including some seriously goofy ones. While oddities from around the globe are fun to explore and explain, it is a little more relevant to touch on road signs that crop up on U.S. soil. Some of these will no doubt be familiar, but others may not, so there is good reason to delve into them and provide a quick explainer. The black "Y" sign was the one that initially piqued our interest, which, by the way, highlights that a Y-shaped intersection is to be found up ahead. That led us to look at other weird letter- and symbol-focused road signs drivers may come across.

The obvious candidates are "P" for parking areas and "H" that marks hospitals, which are universally understood, but throughout this article, we will also explore what those weird "T" or upturned "U" signs mean, in addition to winding "S" and plus-symbol signs. That includes where drivers are most likely to find them and how best to react when they do. Listed in no particular order, here are our picks of eight letter- or symbol-focused road signs that we thought are worth exploring in a bit more detail.

As already explained briefly above, a sign that depicts a capitalized "Y" is there to let drivers know of a Y-shaped three-way intersection or a forked road ahead. The sign will typically be a diamond shape with a yellow background, which means it's a simple warning sign alerting drivers that the road splits. The choices of where to go are obvious — either left or right — but what might be less obvious is how the right of way is determined in a three-way intersection that isn't controlled by any other signs.

The general rule is that drivers must yield to the car on the right. Then there's always the first-to-arrive rule, which works as you would intend — the first to arrive at the split goes first. Of course, some drivers will assume right of way when it is not deserved, or perhaps they just do not see the other car(s). In such instances, it's best to yield to the other vehicle even if you believe you should have the right of way to ensure everyone gets past the intersection without incident.

When a road splits like this, it pays to be especially cautious. Drivers may attempt to dart from one lane to the next when correcting a last-minute wrong decision, plus motorbikes or cyclists could be hiding in your blind spots, so try to ensure you have sufficient space around you if possible. The NHTSA highlights that roughly one third of all traffic fatalities occur at signposted intersections, so doubling down on cautiousness here is never a bad idea, no matter how simple it may first appear.

Surprise surprise, the letter "T" on a road sign warns of a T-shaped intersection — we would hope that you managed to work that one out. The road you are traveling on is about to end and merge with another. Once again, this is a classic warning sign finished in a yellow and black colorway, on a diamond-shaped sign that lets drivers know something's coming up and they will have to act accordingly.

In this instance, drivers must yield to traffic on the intersecting road — they don't have the right of way here — before heading either left or right. There will usually be a stop or yield sign prior to the intersection itself, and to avoid any frustrating bumps with other drivers making late decisions, it's best to get into the required lane early rather than last-minute. Thankfully, of the deadliest intersections on U.S. roads, not many are T-shaped, but the same rules as above apply here. Give yourself space, keep an eye on your blind spots, and don't rush. Oh, and don't forget to use your turn signals either, even if you are in a dedicated turn lane.

Again, we won't be handing out prizes to anyone who is able to correctly guess this one. A cross or "plus" symbol indicates that a crossroad is coming up, and just like the other aforementioned sign, this too will be depicted on a black and yellow diamond-shaped warning sign. Here, traffic can and likely will be coming from all directions, so it's imperative to take the time that's needed and approach it with care. Be aware that drivers on the opposite leg might try to cross ahead of you as you pull out, and be sure to check traffic from both left and right directions. As always, make sure to follow the instructions depicted on any regulatory signs posted in your direction, as these might detail give-way instructions.

There is a good case to make here in favor of the roundabout. In many ways, it is a far better design than the traditional intersection, with fewer conflict points, no left-turns across the flow of traffic, and lower speeds. Roundabouts might seem perplexing to some, but according to data from the Federal Highway Administration, they have been proven to reduce injury and fatal crashes by 76% and 90%, respectively, plus they are far more time-efficient than traffic signals, so perhaps road users ought to just get used to them.

OK, that's not strictly true. This sign, which isn't an "S" at all — it's just a winding arrow — signifies that a winding road is up around. In other words, the natural habitat for a Miata owner who's looking for some engaging roads to make the most of their drop-top weekend car. Of course, the sign isn't actually there to tell drivers that the road is a great place to go hooning. Instead, it lets them know the road ahead has several curves one after another and that they should approach that section of road with some caution.

In fact, these "S" signs will frequently be accompanied by another advisory speed sign that suggests the maximum safe speed for the section. Drivers would be wise to pay attention to these speeds, as around 25% of all fatal road incidents happen on a curved section of road, with common causes being cars either drifting off into trees, rocks, or other fixed objects, or indeed drifting across the center of the road and causing a head-on collision with other traffic.

Anyone who can't figure this one out should probably start taking notes here. An upturned U-shaped arrow crossed out in red, of course, means that U-turns are not permitted. Drivers should continue on, take a left or right turn if available, and find another suitable route. Exceptions will not be made for those in classic red-and-white Gran Torinos.

This is the first sign featured on our list that strays from that typical yellow and black warning sign colorway. Instead, this is a rectangular regulatory sign with a white background. These signs can crop up just about anywhere, but they can commonly be spotted in business districts, on narrow streets, or busier stretches of the nation's roads, such as on multi-lane highways and freeways.

For those who may question the legality of U-turns when this sign isn't present, drivers can usually perform such a move in these circumstances: They should be in the leftmost or center-left lane, signalling their intention, and keeping an eye out for traffic as per any other manoeuvre.

A sideways "T" sign indicates that a side road is coming up. Depending on which way the T is oriented, the sign highlights whether the side road is on the right or the left. If the sign instead looks like an F or an E, but with an extended top or bottom, this means that two side roads are coming up on the right-hand side.

Specifically, this sign will appear when the side roads on the right are particularly close to one another. The hazard this poses to drivers on the major road is quite obvious; they should be wary of traffic entering from the right, and should be prepared to slow down. The sign itself is a typical diamond-shaped yellow-and-black warning sign, which does not indicate right of way. Typically, those on the major road will have the right of way, while those on the indicated side roads will have to yield to traffic on the major road.

The sign for a roundabout shows three arrows forming a circle, and once again, it will be a yellow-and-black diamond-shaped warning sign. Roundabouts always run in a counter-clockwise direction in North America. They can have numerous lanes and are an alternative to crossroads. Here, the right of way rules are simple and uniform: Drivers must always give way to traffic on the left — in other words, the cars that are already on the roundabout.

Getting the right lane is crucial for avoiding accidents; the leftmost lane is used for turning left, whereas the rightmost lanes are used for turning right, and both can be used for going straight on. These are the rules on multi-lane roundabouts unless signposted otherwise — this way, traffic in either lane does not cross unnecessarily.

As for whether drivers need to use turn signals on a roundabout or not, the answer is yes. It's never a bad idea to let other drivers know where you are going; even if you are comfortable in your own abilities to get across safely, it's best practice to help other motorists out by indicating to them your next intended move.

Canada Goose: 3 Key Takeaways for Long-Term Investors

📅 Nov 15, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Store traffic is the open question: the retail network is where the apparel gains either hold up or stall.

On its second quarter of fiscal 2027 earnings call, held on Nov. 5, 2026, Canada Goose (GOOS +2.17%) announced a 5% revenue increase to 274 million Canadian dollars while expanding its gross profit margins. Long-term investors need to evaluate whether the luxury outerwear maker can build a sustainable year-round business while addressing headwinds in retail store traffic. Three things management said stand out.

Management expanded gross margins through improved sourcing, manufacturing efficiency, and higher pricing, rather than relying on short-term external boosts. These operational upgrades helped expand gross margins even as retail network costs increased.

Expanding beyond traditional heavy parkas creates a balanced business model that reduces reliance on cold weather. Sales growth in lighter apparel and rainwear indicates that retail customers increasingly purchase these products from the brand year-round.

Lower physical store traffic dragged down direct-to-consumer (DTC) comparable sales, which measure performance at locations open for over a year. Better online sales and store conversion rates partially cushioned the drop, but store foot traffic remains a clear challenge.

Wholesale orders are expanding, and management projects steady progress toward its annual profitability targets. Operational upgrades and pricing discipline support long-term margins while leadership tracks store traffic recovery.

Execution now centers on boosting store traffic and improving retail conversion during the peak winter buying season.

Where was Nvidia’s security team?

📅 Nov 15, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Outsiders had little reason to notice when Nvidia’s longtime chief security officer and head of product security stepped down in August. Just two and a half months later, CEO Jensen Huang himself left the company over growing questions about his extramarital relationships — starting with an executive at Megaspeed, a Singapore data center company that U.S. investigators suspect of funneling Nvidia chips to China, then widening to reports about two separate affairs with Nvidia employees.

At the time, David Reber, who spent four years as CSO and seven at Nvidia overall, said he was leaving for health reasons. “This wasn’t a pivot I had planned. But I know what needs to come first: family and health,” he wrote on LinkedIn in late August, disclosing that he’d been diagnosed with young-onset Parkinson’s more than two years ago.

Reber’s replacement, Saša Zdjelar, previously spent 17 years at ExxonMobil in Houston, leaving as a senior IT manager before spending one year at Salesforce and several years at the cybersecurity company ReversingLabs. Zdjelar didn’t respond to questions Thursday about the transition or what the security team knew.

Nothing public connects Reber’s or Zdjelar’s role changes to the Megaspeed matter, but it seems fair to wonder what Nvidia’s security team knew about the government’s scrutiny of Megaspeed and about Huang’s relationships, and whether anyone raised concerns. Reber didn’t respond to questions Thursday. Nvidia also has not answered our related questions.

Not all of those relationships are the business of the security team. Relationships with subordinates typically fall to HR, legal, and the board. But an affair with an executive at a company under federal investigation is a different animal. (Megaspeed has denied any wrongdoing.) Security specialists say affairs can create vulnerabilities that foreign intelligence services look to exploit.

Catching that kind of exposure isn’t easy. The way intelligence services collect information, much of it through everyday conversation rather than hacking, makes it especially difficult for security teams to identify risks, according to retired senior intelligence officer Nicholas Eftimiades, who says he has trained more than 1,000 corporate security professionals.

Some people inside Nvidia clearly knew parts of the picture. Nvidia’s top lawyer was engaged with the government’s Megaspeed inquiry more than a year ago, The New York Times reported earlier this week. (Nvidia has said it regularly contacts officials.) Reports about Huang’s relationships with employees suggest colleagues also knew of them, even if the behavior is not expressly forbidden by Nvidia’s code of conduct. The Times cited five Nvidia employees who said he was involved with a vice president who reported directly to him, and Reuters, citing two people, later reported a second relationship. What’s unclear is whether any of that reached security.

Even if it had reached Nvidia’s security team, Eftimiades, who wrote Chinese Espionage Operations and Tactics in 2020, said a security team’s resources are often limited. For one thing, they’re often underfunded because, unlike some other cost centers, they don’t drive revenue. It’s astonishingly common for CEOs to answer a security team’s warnings with “not a nickel more than compliance,” he said.

Many companies also define security very narrowly, specialists say. In an opinion piece in The Hill, Don Aviv, CEO of the corporate investigations firm Interfor International, and author Jeremy Hurewitz, wrote, “Too many companies still approach security primarily as a matter of protecting facilities, preventing cyberattacks, and responding to physical threats.” The two argued that “[s]ecurity teams need to pay greater attention to insider threats, including the cultivation of executives and employees by foreign intelligence services, suspicious business relationships, conflicts of interest and attempts to obtain sensitive information through trusted personal contacts.”

Still, some of it is beyond what any team can do. Speaking generally, not about Huang, Eftimiades offered a hypothetical in which a CEO complains that he can’t do something because “I’m fighting a board who doesn’t want to do this.” That tells an intelligence asset who the obstacle is. The service then asks, “Who’s the sticking point here?” and looks for ways into that person’s email and phone.

What a rival intelligence service wants is often less technology than policy insight, including who’s pushing to change export rules and who the biggest blocker is. “Intel world tends to be like cancer,” he said. “Once it gets into the body, it looks to spread and looks to reach out.”

The playbook isn’t new, Eftimiades added, “particularly for China.” Any executive dealing with the country “will tell you that they’ve been approached.” He said there are also “tons of diplomat stories” of U.S. diplomats walking into a hotel room to find a woman on the bed. He said what’s newer compared with Soviet era spying is the relationships these services cultivate now often last years, not weeks.

Technology can augment that human intelligence, Eftimiades said, pointing to IMSI catchers are one example. These devices mimic a cell tower so that nearby phones connect to them. A basic version can cost about $1,000 and fit in a suitcase, he said, though government-grade models cost far more.

Eftimiades says no one knows yet if these were used against Huang, whose phone was presumably hardened such that compromises could be detected, if not stopped altogether. But either way, what Nvidia’s security team knew, and whether it could have acted on it, should become clearer as the board’s independent investigation and federal inquiries proceed.

In the meantime, Eftimiades said he hopes the whole episode is “a wake-up call” for U.S. industry, which he said is mostly “clueless” about China’s tactics. “We have lost trillions [of dollars] in intellectual property,” is his guess. “Whatever we think we’re doing isn’t working.”

Bilt Will Stop Awarding You 4% Bilt Cash Earning At Card Renewal—Unless You Change This App Setting

📅 Nov 15, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

I receive compensation for content and many links on this blog. Be aware that websites may earn compensation when a customer clicks on a link, when an application is approved, or when an account is opened. Citibank is an advertising partner of this site, as is American Express, Chase, Capital One, and others. Any opinions expressed in this post are my own, and have not been reviewed, approved, or endorsed by my advertising partners. I do not write about all offers that are available -- instead focusing on miles, points, and cash back (and currencies that can be converted into the same). Terms apply to the offers and benefits listed on this page.

Bilt Rewards is changing how rewards are earned on their credit cards. Today, cardmembers have a choice:

Earn 4% back in Bilt Cash, in addition to points. That Bilt Cash can be redeemed for points-earning on housing payments, faster points-earning (accelerators) and redemptions on hotel stays or with partners like Blade, Lyft, Walgreens, etc.

Skip Bilt Cash and earn ‘up to 1.25x on housing payments’ based on your month’s spending. Bilt is moving everyone over to the second option, however existing cardmembers and those who apply, are approved and accept their card through December 31 can opt in to preserving #1. It’s possible to do better with earning up to 1.25x on housing payments, but for most people doing significant spend on the card (where they’re earning more Bilt Cash than their housing payment would consume) they’ll come out ahead earning Bilt Cash. And of course you can always choose to move over to the new model later if you prefer! You need to proactively choose continuing to earn Bilt Cash, to avoid moving over. Once you actually move over (starting at your card renewal date) it cannot be reversed. I’ve already protected my Bilt Palladium Card (See rates and fees) with the legacy earning structure. This is the card I use for most of my spending. Here’s where you go in the app to do it. Go to your wallet and select your Bilt card. Then either go to your current choice for Bonus Rewards (e.g. “Flexible Bilt Cash”) underneath Your card rewards or go to Manage card and select bonus rewards which is the last option at the bottom. Select ‘your rewards are updating’ which is the top option on the screen. The default, which is already selected, is the new rewards model. Swipe right… Select ‘flexible Bilt Cash’ and hit save. You have until your card renewal date to make this choice, but if you preserve the old earning model you can always make the (irreversible) switch later. I’ve already made my choice. XRP to $1.3? Kalshi Traders Make Weak November Bets

📅 2026-11-15  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

XRP has continued to fall to levels not seen in the last three months as prolonged market volatility continues to intensify, causing the asset to post bigger daily price losses.

While momentum appears to be fading rapidly, the slow price movements have caused expectations for a November recovery to weaken, especially among prediction market traders.

Latest data from leading prediction market Kalshi shows that XRP traders have little confidence that the asset can still reclaim $1.30 before the end of the month.

While the asset saw higher expectations from Kalshi traders last month, it appears that its recent price dip below $1 has lowered their expectations as traders are assigning low probabilities to XRP reaching higher price targets this month.

Following their predictions, XRP currently has 7% odds of trading above $1.40, while the probability of reaching $1.50 stands at 8%.

More notably, bets on XRP surging to around $1.60 are even weaker, with only a 4% implied probability, suggesting that traders have increasingly lost confidence in XRP’s potential to achieve a major recovery before this month runs out.

The weak XRP predictions from Kalshi traders have come as XRP struggles to maintain momentum around its recent price levels, falling substantially below its local highs.

As of the time of writing, XRP is trading around $0.9892, representing a substantial decline of about 3.94% over the past 24 hours and an 11.3% drop over the last week.

It is important to note that the Kalshi data only reflects traders' positioning amid current market conditions and therefore does not guarantee XRP’s precise price movement in the future.

While XRP has continued to struggle below the major $1 level, it would need to stage a major recovery to reclaim the $1.30 level.

Cowboys vs 49ers: 2 players to buy, 2 to sell against San Francisco

📅 Nov 15, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

The Dallas Cowboys are coming off a narrow win over the Indianapolis Colts, and now the 7-1 San Francisco 49ers are coming to town, bringing one of the league's most complete rosters and a defense that has consistently given Dallas problems in their recent matchups.

There are some opportunities for the Cowboys, particularly with CeeDee Lamb playing at a high level, but there are also some difficult matchups that could expose weaknesses on Dallas' roster. Here are two players to buy and two to sell ahead of Sunday's game.

I mean, who else would this list start with? Lamb has been the best player for the Cowboys all season, and there's no reason to think that Dallas will stop feeding him the ball. Lamb is coming off a monster performance against Indianapolis, hauling in 10 catches for 195 yards and a touchdown on 16 targets. It was his second-highest receiving total in a game during his career.

Through nine games, Lamb has 70 receptions for 904 yards and seven touchdowns. He's already surpassed the 900-yard mark, and his 108 targets lead the Cowboys by a wide margin. The matchup against San Francisco is difficult, but Lamb is the kind of receiver who can change a game regardless of the opponent. With George Pickens possibly missing another week and Dallas dealing with inconsistencies elsewhere the offense, Dak Prescott should continue looking Lamb's way early and often.

The 49ers will undoubtedly have a plan for him, but Lamb's recent production makes him difficult to leave out of the buyers market. He's been the Cowboys' most reliable playmaker overall, and Dallas needs to find ways to get him involved.

At some point, the Cowboys have to figure out how to beat San Francisco, and Dak Prescott has to be a big part of that solution. Unfortunately, his history against the 49ers doesn't inspire much confidence. Prescott is 2-4 against San Francisco in his career, including an 0-2 record in the playoffs. In those two postseason games, he has thrown for 460 yards, two touchdowns and three interceptions. His most recent regular-season meetings haven't been much better. He threw three interceptions in the 2023 blowout loss and two more in the 2024 matchup.

Across his four regular-season games against San Francisco, Prescott has completed 65.5% of his passes for 875 yards, eight touchdowns and five interceptions. That's not necessarily disastrous production, but the turnovers have been costly, particularly against a team that knows how to capitalize on mistakes.

The 49ers have won four straight meetings against Dallas, and Prescott will have to overcome both that team history and his own struggles against this opponent. San Francisco's defense is disciplined, physical and capable of making quarterbacks pay for forcing throws.

With Fred Warner leading the unit, Prescott will have to be sharp with his reads and decision-making, especially against the blitz, where he has looked terrible this year. Dallas needs a big performance from its quarterback to pull off the upset. Given the history, though, this is a matchup where I'm selling Prescott until he proves otherwise.

With Jake Ferguson ruled out due to a knee injury, Brevyn Spann-Ford has an opportunity to take on an even bigger role in the Cowboys' offense. It might not be the most obvious matchup to exploit, but he could be a sneaky contributor against San Francisco.

Spann-Ford has caught 15 passes for 130 yards this season, and his role has been growing. Against Indianapolis, he played 55 offensive snaps compared to Ferguson's 27, catching two of three targets for 20 yards. That workload suggests the Cowboys are already comfortable giving him significant playing time.

The 49ers have plenty of defensive talent to account for, and their attention will naturally gravitate toward Lamb and the Cowboys' other primary playmakers. That could leave opportunities for Spann-Ford underneath or in the middle of the field, particularly if Dallas uses him to help with protection before releasing him into a route.

This isn't a prediction that Spann-Ford is suddenly going to become the focal point of the passing game. Rather, it's a bet that his increased playing time and the attention paid to Dallas' other weapons could create a few favorable opportunities. With Ferguson unavailable, the Cowboys need someone else to contribute from the tight end position. Spann-Ford is in a good position to do that.

The Cowboys have indicated that Jaishawn Barham could see an increased workload as injuries continue to pile up on the defensive side of the ball. That's understandable from a personnel standpoint, but it also creates a potential problem against an offense as versatile as San Francisco's.

Barham had some encouraging moments early in the season, but his limitations have become more apparent as the competition has increased. His playing time has also been limited recently, which makes the prospect of a larger role against the 49ers particularly concerning.

San Francisco is exactly the type of opponent that can put a young linebacker in difficult situations. Kyle Shanahan's offense uses motion, misdirection and mismatches to create space, and open up running lanes.

If Dallas gives him more responsibility, San Francisco will have plenty of opportunities to test him. The Cowboys need all the help they can get defensively, but this is a tough week to ask an inconsistent young player to take on a bigger role.

Danny Boyle Says Tabloid Headlines Censored In British Version Of ‘Ink’ Will Be Shown In U.S.

📅 Nov 14, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Ink director Danny Boyle on Saturday confirmed that redacted Sun newspaper headlines shown as part of an epilogue in his Netflix drama about the founding of the UK tabloid will be shown in full in the U.S. theatrical and streaming version of the film.

Speaking while receiving the Deadline Legend Awards during Deadline’s Contenders Film Los Angeles awards-season event Saturday, Boyle said an edited version showing the headlines is currently being edited into a new cut of the film in time for its December 11 release in select theaters.

The pic, based on James Graham’s play and adapted screenplay that tells the story of Rupert Murdoch and editor Larry Lamb’s takeover of the British tabloid The Sun in the late 1960s, setting the tone for new kind of journalism in the process, was unable to show the headlines owing to Britain’s stringent libel laws.

“We’ve had a battle to get the film to where it is now, and I can tell you — I can say this, can’t I? — I think that we just won the ability [to show the headlines] because we’re in America, and you have better protection for freedom of speech than we have in Britain,” Boyle said during the Q&A session at the DGA Theater, adding that he hopes the uncensored version “will be [in] the finished version of the film, which we’ve never been able to show in Britain.” Watch on Deadline

Boyle pointed to one specific headline in the epilogue that was redacted in UK versions of the pic, above an article about the 1989 death of 97 fans crushed while trying to enter Hillsborough Stadium to watch a Liverpool-Nottingham Forest FA Cup soccer game. The tabloid report blamed Liverpool fans for the tragedy, citing what turned out to fabricated police and official reports.

“There are various headlines … at the end of [Ink], and there’s one particular, which although it won’t mean anything to you, to anybody from Britain it does mean a great deal, which is that The Sun ran a terrible headline called ‘The Truth,’ which is one of the principal concerns of James Graham’s screenplay throughout the whole film,” Boyle said.“[The Sun] ran this headline, and it was a bunch of lies about the way that Liverpool fans were killed at the Hillsborough disaster,” he continued. “It’s one of the low points of British journalism, and we wanted that to be always in the film, and we weren’t able to have it in the British version. We have these redacted versions, but know that we’re in America. We’ve got it in there, and I hope that the tidal wave will then reach back to Britain, and we’ll be allowed to put them in there because it’s an important moment for freedom of speech, which you have enshrined.”

He added, “You know, it’s it’s it’s interesting that a tabloid that you know that lets it all hang out would try to stop you from doing something similar.”

The epilogue in the pic, which stars Jack O’Connell as Lamb and Guy Pearce as Murdoch, is also key as it ties The Sun’s rise to modern tabloids — and Murdoch’s eventual founding of Fox News.

“What they ignited … and the pact that they came up with to start The Sun culminates financially and principally in the birth of Fox News,” Boyle said. “Because the money that The Sun made allowed Murdoch to buy 20th Century Fox and then to finance the establishment, the extraordinary establishment, of a new terrestrial channel in America, which takes a lot of money. So there’s an umbilical cord, I think, that connects these two guys and their meeting in 1969 with where we are now.”

Ink, which has been hitting festival since its world premiere to open this year’s Venice Film Festival, is due to open in the UK on January 8, when it becomes available to stream on Netflix.

Liverpool transfer news: 'Significant' interest in £29m centre-back as Andoni Iraola ponders future without Virgil van Dijk

📅 Nov 14, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

By Lewis Nolan | 14 Nov 2026 23:43

Liverpool are keen on Lens centre-back Pierre Ismaelo Ganiou despite interest from a number of other clubs, the latest report has claimed.

The Premier League is now on pause for the November international break, and that has given Reds supporters the chance to assess the squad.

Andoni Iraola has made a positive start to life at Anfield, with the team's defence having notably improved, led by the likes of Virgil van Dijk and Ronald Araujo.

However, Van Djik has entered the final year of his contract, while Araujo is only on loan from Barcelona and could return to the Spanish side at the end of the season.

Amid uncertainty regarding the future of their defence, Football Insider report that the Reds are one of several clubs keen on signing Lens defender Ganiou, who is valued by Transfermarkt at just over £25m.

Ganiou does not have an extensive history of top-flight football considering he has played just 28 games in Ligue 1 in his entire career, though he has been impressive for Lens.

In five league matches for the French side this season, the 21-year-old has won 69% of his duels, including 73% of his ground duels.

Liverpool have typically looked to sign aerially dominant defenders, so while the Frenchman boasts an impressive 64% duel success rate in the air, he only averages 1.8 aerial duels won per game.

Van Dijk has won an average of 3.8 duels in the air in the league so far, while Jeremy Jacquet has won 2.6 aerial duels in the Premier League, and the Reds would almost certainly aim to get Ganiou closer to those figures.

While Van Dijk is 35 and is into the final year of his contract, he has shown no signs of physical decline, and he has expressed a desire to remain at Anfield.

If he was to extend his stay on Merseyside, the Reds may not need to delve into the market for another centre-back.

Joe Gomez is expected to leave, and he could be replaced in the squad by Araujo, which would then leave the club in need of a right-back.

Jacquet and Giovanni Leoni will be part of the squad for the coming seasons, while many believe that Ifeanyi Ndukwe could play a role for the team once his loan at Levante ends.

Investing - Theory, News &amp; General • Re: Do you follow a Ferri/Bernstein/Swenson/other portfolio?

📅 Nov 14, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →



That's fascinating to me coming from you. So the whole concern over being able to harvest the foreign tax credits is overplayed in your opinion?
I would not use the word overplayed, because that assigns a subjective value. When I've done the math, the difference in total tax cost (VT vs similarly-weighted allocation of VTI + VXUS) is a single-digit number of basis points. For me, that's worth it for the convenience and behavioral finance benefit of not needing to rebalance. "I would have no qualms about it," as I said. Somebody else could decide that it's a big enough difference that they'd absolutely prefer the multiple-fund approach.
Interesting thanks. And since I started using a CPA a few years back, that savings might be even less. TBH, I don't know exactly how they decide what to charge me, but adding another form (to recover the foreign tax credit) probably doesn't make my tax return bill cheaper.

Investing - Theory, News &amp; General • Re: Why personal finance isn't taught, and, how to teach it to kids

📅 Nov 14, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

I think talking about money and normalizing discussing it is the first step. It shouldn't be this hush hush taboo topic. It is a part of everyone's everyday life and the earlier a child learns that the better. Start with simple things and move on to more complicated as they get older. I look forward to teaching my child about compound interest when she is mature enough to comprehend it. I have a few more years to wait.... For now I am happy to ask her how much something costs when she asks for it at the grocery store for example. Associating that there is a cost/desire for an item rather than just a desire is a simple start. Want that box of cheese its? How much does it cost etc. She knows I have "accounts" for her but she has no concept of what kind of accounts. She will know that she should always "save, invest, and spend" in that order. There is still plenty of time and my goal is that she is more financially literate than I ever was at her age. Not a major feat since I never even heard of an Roth Ira till my late 30's. I am very late to the game. She will be better prepared at a younger age.

This Playful Florida Raised Ranch Is Proof That Bold Style Can Be Incredibly Timeless

📅 Nov 14, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Classic materials and a modern layout read as "traditional with a twist."

How do you turn a straightforward ranch-style home into something exciting and unexpected yet timeless? When a young family of four asked designer James Farmer to do just that for their central Florida abode, he opted for a gut renovation, using his homegrown knowledge of Southern style, years of interior expertise, and pointed enthusiasm to renovate ranches to create a highly functional and striking escape for his clients.

Located in an old, established neighborhood on a lake in Winter Park, Florida, the home was surrounded by mature live oaks and beautiful landscaping. “I would describe the area as old-school Florida with a touch of Mayberry,” says Farmer, referencing the fictional town from The Andy Griffith Show. The home itself was humble and honest—“no hiding its hand at the poker table,” says Farmer.

Alongside Skolfield Homes, Farmer kept the architectural style generally the same, with the most impactful changes being a complete overhaul of the galley kitchen and a relocation of the staircase from the side of the house to the middle, putting it in its deserved place as a focal point of the living room. The new kitchen is what he calls a “glorified galley,” with a sunny breakfast room off to the side that used to be a screened-in porch. He then reinforced the home as a "front door house," ensuring the formal entry space would actually be used rather than passed through by adding a bar perfect for fancy cocktail parties or a casual post-work martini.

For the actual interior, the designer layered various textures and bold patterns, combining them with hallmarks of grand Floridian style, like floral wallpapers, gold details, intricate trim, palm motifs, pecky cypress wallcoverings, and a coastal color palette. The line between indoor and outdoor living is further blurred with trellis details and a selection of timeworn brick and wood flooring. "We really leaned into this in a new way with this project because of its location," he says. "True, classic Florida can be so fun but also timeless, and that was our goal with this project."

Farmer encouraged his clients to splurge only on materials and pieces that'll truly stand the test of time, opting for classic finishes and prints that will age with the young family—an approach he details intimately in his newest book, Home Again: A Return to Gracious Interiors. Ultimately, the decadent interior paired with the modern structural changes naturally created what ended up being Farmer's motto for the home: "Traditional with a twist!"

FAST FACTSDesigner: James Farmer of James Farmer DesignsLocation: Winter Park, FloridaThe Space: A ranch-style home with 4 bedrooms and 3.5 bathrooms

Fabulous cocktail parties start in this formal space before migrating to other parts of the house. With no TV in the space, all eyes are drawn to the bar set behind a custom set of accent chairs by Thibaut, which is where Farmer's clients shake up (not stir) "the best martinis in Florida."

A color palette of peach, robin's egg blue, and butterscotch is celebrated in this room, the designer explains. By painting the ceiling in a blue tone—a trick commonly seen on Southern porches—he gave the room a subtle indoor-outdoor touch commonly seen on Southern porches.

Borrowed light from the kitchen and stairwell shines on the rich velvets and great patterns in this family room. “The color combo of terracotta and emerald with creamy white millwork creates a handsome and elegant palette,” Farmer adds.

Once a screened-in porch, Farmer kept the alfresco feel by covering the walls and ceiling with trelliage panels and outfitting the room with timeless white wicker furniture by Memoire Design (once known as Mainly Baskets Home). Vintage elephant garden stools now stand as whimsical side tables.

“We kept the original corner cabinets and wainscoting but gave it a bit of glam with a custom Gracie Studio wallpaper and lacquered ceiling,” Farmer says of the dining room, which kept its original footprint. The console tables are painted in a lemony yellow hue to match the window treatments and the room's other cheerful elements.

Farmer broke up this completely new, double galley kitchen into sections to make hosting a breeze. On one side of the counter is the seating and hangout zone, while the other side plays host to a functional working kitchen, outfitted with a Wolf range and Sub-Zero fridge. Think of it like traditional with a twist; "These clients are fabulous entertainers, and the island can also make a great buffet for parties," he says. "We like to think that The Golden Girls would thrive in this kitchen… Mid-century meets Maw-Maw!"

Old Chicago brick and palm motifs in the wallpaper make this breakfast hub feel more like a terrace than an interior enclave. Farmer created this room as part of the kitchen expansion—its bay window only enhances the porch-like vibe he wanted. "Sometimes geometry is your best friend," Farmer says, noting that the rug, table, and even shape of the room are octagonal. "You might not notice it, but it makes a moment."

“We were able to move the staircase in this house, which allowed for a more efficient floor plan for this young family,” he explains. “When you are under a major renovation, you can think outside of the box, or sometimes inside the box, just as easily.” Now the stairwell can live as a beautiful moment all its own at the center of the home, rather than pushed to the side.

The daughter was sleeping in a crib when Farmer first started working on his clients' house, so he wanted to design a room that could grow with her (by the time the project wrapped, she was in her big girl bed). The pink trellis wallpaper by Schumacher perfectly rides the line between feminine and elevated.

Touches of bamboo, palm, and pecky cypress introduce that old Florida feel, reminiscent of family vacations to the family's favorite spots in the Sunshine State, without being too on the nose.

“An interior design trick that is so old-school, but still so fun, is using a drapery fabric for wall covering and vice versa,” Farmer explains. “We were able to bring that from the primary bedroom to the primary bathroom.”

James Farmer is a Southern author, interior designer, and speaker known for his ability to create beautifully familiar and welcoming homes. To James, a home engages all of the senses—the pleasantries of colors and materials, the feel of the doorknob warmed by the sun, the smell of fresh laundry, the sound of the closing door, and the taste of supper on the table. Through his design firm, James works with clients to truly create a home.

Projects by James Farmer Designs can be found from the countryside of Connecticut to James's home state of Georgia. Though his work can be found throughout the country, James proudly has built his business in his hometown of Perry, Georgia.

‘Magical’ Tree House With 30-Foot Glass Living Room Returns to the Market

📅 Nov 14, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

A “magical” four-bedroom retreat encased in glass has returned to the market in Austin, TX, for $1,095,000.

The Glass Tree House debuted on the market for $1.5 million in August 2024 and has received several price cuts since then.

Custom slate stairs lead to a jaw-dropping living room with 30-foot-tall windows and custom rock walls carved into a cliffside, with tree-lined views that make you feel at one with nature.

“When you walk in, it feels like wherever you go, there is so much privacy and the view is very unique,” says listing agent Jenny Law of Kuper Sotheby’s International Realty. “It reminds me of a modern tree house that takes you far away from city life.”

Built in 2008, the 2,180-square-foot metal-clad contemporary was designed by architect John Allen to blur the boundaries between the indoor living spaces and the outdoor setting.

“The sellers use it as a short-term rental property,” Law notes. “It’s a nice retreat and would be a popular second home for someone because of its views and location, or it could be used as a primary residence for its school system.”

The sleek kitchen has custom black cabinetry, honey onyx countertops, built-in seating, and stainless-steel appliances.

The glass-surrounded primary suite has a soaking tub, a moss rock shower, Calacatta marble countertops, marble flooring, and access to a private terrace.

“It’s located in Apache Shores, where there are not a lot of restrictions in that area,” Law says. “It’s about 15 to 20 minutes from downtown Austin, but you feel like you are out in nature with these nice hill country views.”

A second lot, which could allow for a future pool, workshop, or guesthouse with applicable approvals, is available for separate purchase.

The property is also minutes from Lake Travis and Lake Austin, a residents-only boat launch, an 18-hole disc golf course, a soccer field, a community hall, a swimming pool, and sports courts.

“In Austin, there are so many homes that look the same, but this one is unique and beautiful,” Law says. “You truly feel like you are living outside.”
